The outline in plain words

Argumenttikartta · lähde: /Users/markomanninen/Documents/GitHub/riemann-hypothesis/verification/skeleton/READING.md

Tämä tiedosto generoidaan. Muokkaa verkkoa READING.graph.json ja yksiköintiä READING.units.json, ja piirrä kartta uudelleen skriptillä render.py.
Solmutyypit ja runkolajit (D määritelmä, A aksiooma tai lähtökohta, T teoreema johtoineen, E esimerkki tai havainto, O oletus tai kehys): Väite Oletus Peruste Määritelmä Lause Esimerkki Sitaatti Tulos Vastaus

0 · The outline in plain words

r-top · r. 3 Väite T

The top line of the outline is a theorem of Main.lean which states that the value DirichletCharacter.LFunction χ s of Mathlib is not zero for real part above 1/2, except for the trivial character at 1

The top line of the outline is a theorem of Main.lean. It states that the value DirichletCharacter.LFunction χ s of the Mathlib library is not zero for every DirichletCharacter χ of that library and every complex number s with real part above 1/2, except when χ is the trivial character and s is 1.

Johto: L1-states · L1-place · t-scope (todistus seuraa tulosta) lim-what → rajaa
r-38 · r. 3 lahtokohta A

The 37 lines below the top state the steps that gather at least 10,000 declarations, and the document says for each of the 38 lines what its Lean statement claims

The 37 lines below it state the largest steps that the proof passes through, which are the ones that gather at least 10,000 declarations in the graph of the README. This document says for each of the 38 lines what its Lean statement claims, in plain sentences and in ordinary notation.

r-made · r. 11 lahtokohta A

A language model wrote the readings, further runs of the same model checked them, no mathematician has checked them, and each reading keeps what a name only suggests in a separate paragraph

The readings were written by a language model and checked by further runs of the same model. No mathematician has checked them. Each reading has a separate paragraph for what a name only suggests, and the check looked for meanings asserted outside that paragraph.

r-conf · r. 15 Määritelmä D

High confidence means that the reader saw the statement and every project definition in it, medium that some names stayed unopened, and low that it saw little more than a chain of named conditions

The model run that wrote a reading also rated its confidence. High means that it saw the statement and what every project definition in it says. Medium means that it saw the form of the claim with some names left unopened. Low means that it saw little more than a chain of named conditions.

r-confcount · r. 15 Väite T

One line is rated high, 24 medium and 13 low

One line is rated high, 24 are rated medium and 13 are rated low.

r-source · r. 20 lahtokohta A

README.md defines the vocabulary, and the readings are of the source after the merge of 2026-11-22

README.md defines the outline, its lines, the head and the wrappers of a line and the verb gathers. The readings are of the source after the merge of upstream main on 2026-11-22.

1 · What the top of the outline says

t-scope · r. 26 lahtokohta A

A reading covers the statement of the head of a line and neither the proof nor the wrappers, and the sentences of this section about proofs and about Row come from the source files

The numbers are the line numbers of the outline, and every name in code font is a name of the source. A reading covers the statement of the head of a line. It covers neither the proof nor the statements of the wrappers. The sentences below about a proof, about the wrapper of line 1 and about the names of Row come from the source files, which the model session that wrote this document read directly.

t-premise · r. 32 Määritelmä D

A premise of a line is a hypothesis that its theorem takes before its named conclusion

A premise of a line is a hypothesis that its theorem takes before its named conclusion. A condition inside the conclusion is not counted as a premise.

t-l1 · r. 35 Väite T

Line 1 has no premises and states EveryCharacter, that the value LFunction χ s is not zero for real part above 1/2, and its wrapper in Main.lean has the same definition word for word

Line 1 has no premises. Its head everyCharacter states the proposition that the code calls EveryCharacter. For every N ≥ 1, every DirichletCharacter χ of level N and every complex number s with real part above 1/2, the value LFunction χ s is not zero, unless χ is the trivial character and s is 1. The wrapper of the line is the theorem of Main.lean, and its proposition DirichletLNonvanishingRightOfHalf in Statements.lean has word for word the same definition.

Johto: t-scope · L1-states · L1-place (todistus seuraa tulosta)
t-l2 · r. 41 Väite T

Line 2 derives EveryCharacter from one premise, Row, which says that for every CM number field, every prime and every π and χ that meet the conditions IsRegAlg and PolarizedPair a homomorphism into invertible matrices over the field the code calls Qbar l exists with a condition PropsB

Line 2 hangs from line 1 and derives EveryCharacter from one premise. The statement of line 2 writes that premise as Row0b applied to the condition IsRecT. The code names the same proposition Row, and line 33 writes it as Row0b recT, where recT is the code's name for that condition. The definitions show the form of Row. For every CM number field E, every prime l, every a ≥ 1, every π of the code's type CuspSub that meets IsRegAlg and every χ of the type HChar for which the pair meets PolarizedPair, there exist a homomorphism r into the invertible a by a matrices over the field that the code calls Qbar l, an integer and a function HT that meet a condition PropsB. The statement also quantifies over an algebraic closure of E and an isomorphism from Qbar l to ℂ, which this summary leaves out.

The names suggest Galois representations attached to automorphic representations, which the Lean text does not show

The names suggest Galois representations attached to automorphic representations, which the Lean text that the reading was made from does not show.

Line 33 hangs from line 1 and states Row0b recT with no premises. The proof of the head of line 1 in MainConclusion.lean applies the theorem of line 2 to the theorem of line 33.

t-l3 · r. 56 Väite T

Line 3 derives EveryCharacter from a bundle of three fields, two of them Row and one RecTExists, and from VWin, which asks VWinAt ψ 401 for every primitive character

Lines 3 and 30 hang from line 2. Line 3 derives EveryCharacter from a bundle of three fields and a proposition VWin. Two fields of the bundle are Row. The other field is RecTExists, which says that for every number field and every prime of it a map exists that meets a condition IsRecT. VWin says that a condition VWinAt ψ 401 holds for every primitive Dirichlet character ψ.

t-l5 · r. 68 Väite T

Line 5 concludes that a record HypsAlt exists from six of the premises of line 4 and one more, and line 23 concludes ZeroFreeAll from such a record, VWin and Green

Lines 5 and 23 hang from line 4. Line 5 concludes that a record of the type HypsAlt S0.NL DefiniteSideCondition exists. Its premises are six of the seven premises of line 4 and one more. Its premises are the existence of such a record, VWin and a statement Green.

t-6-22 · r. 73 Väite T

Lines 6 to 22 hang below line 5. The readers saw definitions up to three steps away from the statement, as the next section describes. A person can follow which named condition follows from which, and the mathematics behind the names stays closed.

t-hang · r. 79 Väite T

A line without premises can have lines below it, and a lower line can have premises that the upper line lacks, which the proof of the upper line has to supply in a way the readings do not show

By the README, a line hangs below another when the five theorems depend on the lower line only by way of the upper one. A line without premises can therefore still have lines hanging below it. A lower line can also have premises that the line above it lacks, as line 3 has RecTExists and VWin. The proof of the upper line then has to supply them, and the readings do not show how, since they cover statements only.

Johto: t-nopremise · t-scope · t-l3

2 · How the readings were made

m-dossier · r. 88 lahtokohta A

dossier.py wrote for each head a dossier with the statement, the wrappers and definitions at three levels, up to 45 definitions, and the 38 dossiers hold 1,622 definitions

dossier.py wrote a dossier for the head of each line. A dossier holds the Lean statement, the statements of the wrappers of the line, and definitions at three levels. Level one holds the definitions that the statement names, level two those that the definitions of level one name, and level three those that the definitions of level two name. A dossier stops after three levels or after 45 definitions, whichever comes first. Definitions of the first two levels are shown with their bodies and those of the third level without. The 38 dossiers hold 1,622 definitions and about one million characters.

lim-cut → rajaa
m-readers · r. 95 lahtokohta A

A first reader wrote the reading from the dossier alone with a separate field for suggestions, and a second reader wrote its own without seeing the first

A first reader wrote, from the dossier alone, what the theorem states, its premises in order, its conclusion, the statement in ordinary notation, the definitions it relied on, and its confidence. It wrote in a separate field what the names suggest beyond the text.

A second reader wrote its own reading from the same dossier without seeing the first.

m-check · r. 99 lahtokohta A

A check compared both readings with the Lean text, errors were corrected and checked again, all roles are runs of Claude Code in folders holding only the dossiers, and the printed reading is the first reading after correction

A check compared both readings with the Lean text. It looked for a missing or an added premise, a wrong quantifier, a wrong number or exponent, a wrong conclusion, and a meaning asserted for a name whose body the dossier does not show.

A first reading with an error was corrected and checked again.

The first reader, the second reader, the check and the corrections are runs of the command line tool Claude Code. Each run was started in a folder that held only the dossiers and, for the check, the two readings. The briefs of the four roles are in briefs/.

The reading printed for a line is the first reading after correction. The second readings served for the comparison and were left as they were.

m-errors · r. 111 lahtokohta A

The check found 16 errors in the first readings of ten lines and four in the second readings of three, none changed a list of premises or the name of a conclusion, and all 38 printed readings passed after correction

The check found 16 errors in the first readings of ten lines and four errors in the second readings of three lines. Each error was in the detail inside a premise or a conclusion. A reading called a type a group although the dossier does not show that it is one, left out a condition inside a premise, or gave a wrong count. No error changed the list of premises of a line or the name of its conclusion. After correction all 38 printed readings passed the check. Eight of the ten passed after one round, line 7 after two and line 10 after three. The last corrections of lines 7 and 10 are the exception to the separate runs. The model session that wrote this document made them directly and removed the detail that the check had faulted, and the check then passed both.

lim-same → rajaa · m-gemini → tarkistaa
m-gemini · r. 121 lahtokohta A

Gemini repeated the check with 71 findings on 20 readings, none showing a reading that the Lean text contradicts, 16 lines were changed in 40 places, and after a further check and five corrections the changed readings passed

A model of another maker then repeated the check. Gemini, run in the Antigravity editor, read the same dossiers and the printed readings with the brief of the check and faulted 20 of the 38 readings with 71 findings. Each finding was compared with the words of the reading and with the Lean text of the dossier by further runs of Claude Code. No finding showed a reading that states what the Lean text contradicts. In 21 cases the summary of a premise left out a quantifier, an argument or a hypothesis without saying so, and in 16 cases the wording was inexact, with an unbound variable, a dropped argument or a claim stronger than the dossier shows. In 17 cases the reading uses an ordinary word for an object whose body the dossier does not show, and 23 findings did not hold, most of them because another field of the same reading gives the detail said to be missing. The 16 lines with cases of the first two kinds were changed in 40 places, and an entry says so when it was changed. A summary of a premise now names what it leaves out. The 16 changed readings were then checked once more by runs of Claude Code, each in a folder with one dossier and one reading. Twelve passed. In the other four the check found five errors, four of them ordinary words for names without a shown body and one a premise of the wrapper theorems of line 8 that the changed text had left out. The five were corrected with the text of the check, and a second check of those four readings found no error. The other cases of ordinary words stand as they were.

tarkistaa → m-errors
m-entry · r. 139 Määritelmä D

The order of an entry is the place of the head, the statement, premises and conclusion, a display of the statement in ordinary notation, the unopened names, the suggestion from names, the confidence and the result of the check

An entry below shows, in this order, where the head lies, what the theorem states, its premises and its conclusion, a display of the statement in ordinary notation, the names that the reading does not open, what the names suggest, the confidence with its reason, and what the check found. A reading can say that definitions were left out, which refers to the limit of 45 definitions of its dossier.

3 · A deeper reading of lines 6 to 22

dp-how · r. 147 lahtokohta A

The first readings of lines 6 to 22 stayed at the level of names. After the record D7 was opened in D7.md, each of these 17 lines was read once more by a run that saw the dossier of the line, the whole Lean text of D7 with the 282 definitions below it, and the ten readings of GLOSSARY.md. The briefs are briefs/reread-reader.txt and briefs/reread-check.txt.

The 17 deeper readings open 107 names, and the unopened names fall from 180 to 124

dp-errors · r. 153 lahtokohta A

The check found 43 errors in 16 deeper readings and 46 more in 15 after correction, mostly words of mathematics used for names whose body no file shows

They also say more, and the check found more to fault. It found 43 errors in 16 of the 17 readings. After those were corrected with the text that the check supplied, a second check found 46 further errors in 15 readings, and they were corrected in the same way. Most errors of the second round are of one kind. The reading uses a word of mathematics, such as irreducible or unitary, for a name whose body no file shows.

dp-gemini → tarkistaa
dp-gemini · r. 160 lahtokohta A

Gemini made 106 findings on 16 deeper readings, 103 held and none is a sentence that the Lean text contradicts, the weightiest in lines 8 and 18, and all 103 passages were replaced

Gemini then checked the deeper readings as it checked the printed ones. It found line 13 faithful and made 106 findings on the other 16 readings. Runs of Claude Code compared each finding with the Lean text. Three findings did not hold and 103 did. None of the 103 is a sentence that the Lean text contradicts. In 50 the reading uses a word of mathematics for a name whose body no file shows, in 31 the wording is inexact, and in 22 a quantifier, an argument or a hypothesis is left out without a note. The weightiest are in line 8, where the reading stated two bounds without the hypotheses under which the Lean text places them, and in line 18, where it named a density theorem for a statement of existence. All 103 passages were replaced with text that the run checked against the Lean text.

The 16 changed deeper readings were then checked again, each by a run of Claude Code in a folder with its dossier, the text of D7, the glossary and the reading. Fourteen passed. In lines 12 and 22 the check found one error each, a word of mathematics for a name whose body no file shows, and both were corrected with the text of the check. A further check of the two corrected readings found no error. As a test of the check itself, two numbers of the deeper reading of line 8 were changed to wrong ones in a copy, and the check found both. The check and the readings come from one maker's models, and Gemini has not read the corrected texts.

4 · What stays unopened

u-def · r. 183 Määritelmä D

A name is unopened in a reading when the dossier did not let the reader see in full what its definition says, as with RecTExists, whose body rests on IsRecT without a body

A name is unopened in a reading when the first reader marked that the dossier did not let it see in full what the definition says. RecTExists is an example. Its body is shown, and the body rests on IsRecT, which is shown without a body.

5 · Limits

lim-cut · r. 208 lahtokohta A

A dossier stops after three levels or 45 definitions, statements and bodies are cut at fixed lengths, and a reading says where its text was cut

A dossier stops after three levels or after 45 definitions, and the statement splitter keeps at most 3,000 characters of a statement and 2,000 of a body. A reading says where the text it saw was cut.

lim-head · r. 211 lahtokohta A

A reading covers the statement of the head and not the wrappers

A reading covers the statement of the head of a line. It does not cover the statements of the wrappers folded into the line.

The readers and the check are the same language model, so they can share a blind spot that the check does not find

The first reader, the second reader and the check are the same language model, so they can share a blind spot that the check does not find.

A reading says what a statement claims, and neither why it holds nor whether it is the statement that the mathematics needs

A reading says what a statement claims. It does not say why the statement holds, and it does not say whether the statement is the one that the mathematics needs.

6 · Files

7 · How to read the table and the entries

g-how · r. 231 Määritelmä D

In the table the dots give the depth of a head, Gathers counts what the line gathers, and a cell ending in three dots is cut

In the table, the dots before a head give its depth, and a line hangs from the nearest line above it that has one dot fewer. The column Gathers gives the number of declarations that the line gathers, which for a line with wrappers is the number that its first wrapper gathers. A cell that ends in three dots is cut.

Premise 2. ClozelCohomological holds, which says that for every totally complex number field E, every a ≥ 1 and every π of type CuspSub E a, the condition IsRegAlg implies the condition IsCohomological.

Premise 3. HeckeLAdicCharacter holds, which says that for a number field E with algebraic closure Ω there is a map art from Ideles E to Gab E Ω with IsGlobalArtin such that, for every prime l, every isomorphism ι from Qbar l to ℂ and every map n from the ring homomorphisms E → ℂ to ℤ, every χ of type HChar E with IsAlgPos χ n has a unique continuous character r into the units of Qbar l with IsLAdicOf art ι χ n r.

Premise 4. ChebotarevDegreeOne holds, which says that for a finite Galois extension L of a number field F inside an algebraic closure, an automorphism g of L over F and a finite set S of primes, there is a prime v outside S of inertia degree 1 over ℤ with a valuation subring B of Ω that satisfies IsPlaceOver B v and whose subgroup Ine B lies inside the fixing subgroup of L and a σ with IsFrob B (normV v) σ whose restriction to L is g.

Premise 5. CuspUAlt holds, which says that for a CM field E with algebraic closure Ω, a prime l, an isomorphism ι from Qbar l to ℂ, a ≥ 1, π of type CuspSub E a and χ of type HChar on the maximal real subfield of E, with IsRegAlg for π and IsPolarized π χ, there exist a homomorphism r from G E Ω to GL_a(Qbar l), an integer wt and a multiset-valued HT with IsGalUAlt π χ ι r wt HT.

Premise 6. BaseChangeDefiniteUnitaryGroup holds, which unfolds to DefiniteN, saying that for a CM field E unramified at every finite prime over its maximal real subfield, every n ≥ 1 and every π with IsAutRepU there exist c, sizes N_i summing to n, and data V, σ, m with IsBaseChangeU.

Premise 7. BhattScholzeCrystallineLattices holds, a two-part statement under the assumption IsAinf K P₀ e, giving, for every P and AI with IsStd P AI, existence of an object E of type FCrys with a map j satisfying Realises for each lattice in a representation with IsCrys, and, without the IsStd assumption, unique existence of a morphism u of FCrys objects compatible with any equivariant linear map f between modules T and T' over ℤ_[l] that satisfy Realises. This summary leaves out the binders l, K, P₀ and e of the assumption IsAinf K P₀ e.

Premise 8. The condition that the code calls HeckeLAdicCharacterAbove holds, defined as GlobalTheory.TripleGal.CharAbove with no visible body.

Premise 9. CuspRapidDecay holds, which says that for a datum D, a family θ with IsInfFam θ, a V with a map e satisfying IsExpFam θ V e, and a function φ on GA D V with IsAutFun, IsSmoothInf, IsUnifModerate and IsCuspFun, the condition IsRapidDecay holds.

Premise 10. RecTExists holds, which says that for every number field E, algebraic closure Ω, valuation subring A of Ω and prime w of E with IsPlaceOver A w there exists rc with IsRecT h rc.

Premise 11. DiscreteSeriesCover holds, which says that for all naturals p, q with p + q ≥ 1 and every T of type GrpData p q there is a map D from parameters lam with IsParam to DSRep T such that each D(lam) has HasTypes and every R in DSRep T is UnitEquiv to some D(lam).

Premise 12. Conclusion. SurjectiveOntoX holds.
